The [federal budget of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_budget_of_Russia) ([Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language): федеральный бюджет России) is the leading element of the [budget system of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budget_system_of_Russia?action=edit\&redlink=1). It is a major state financial plan for the fiscal year, which has the force of law after its approval by the [Russian parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Assembly_of_Russia) and [signed into law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sign_into_law#Approval) by the [President of Russia](239646.md).

The federal budget is the primary means of redistribution of national income and gross domestic product through it mobilized the financial resources necessary to regulate the country's economic development, social policy and the strengthening of the national defense. The share of federal budget accounts for a significant portion of the distribution process, which is the allocation of funds between sectors of the economy, manufacturing and industrial areas, regions of the country.

The right of the Russian Federation for an independent federal budget is enshrined by Article 71 of the [Russian Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Russia) and the [Budget Code of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budget_Code_of_Russia?action=edit\&redlink=1) that regulates the details of its formation and execution.

### Fiscal policy

See also: [Taxation in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Russia)

Russia was estimated to have a government budget deficit of 2.6% of GDP in 2025, due to falling oil revenue and high military expenditure.[^cite_note-228]

### Debts

See also: [2022 Russian debt default](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russian_debt_default)

Russia is a [creditor country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_net_international_investment_position_per_capita). As the IMF reported in 2016, Russia has a floating exchange rate, large official foreign exchange reserves, a positive net international investment position of about 20 percent of GDP, and a current account surplus.[^cite_note-229] As reported in 2017, despite the low level of foreign direct investment (FDI) and capital market investments, Russia has been a net creditor for most of the last 25 years.[^cite_note-230] As of 2022, Russia is a net creditor on international markets: the value of its foreign assets exceeds the value of its foreign liabilities.[^cite_note-231] Russia has a positive [net international investment position](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_international_investment_position) (NIIP) from December 2013 to September 2024.[^cite_note-232]

Russia has one of the [lowest government debts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_government_debt) (total external and domestic) and [lowest external debts](3001143.md) (total public/government and private) among world's economies.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236]

In 2022, domestic government debt increased by 13.9 percent to 18.78 billion rubles.[^cite_note-237] Russia Domestic government Debt data was reported at RUB 19,801.921 billion in May 2023.[^cite_note-238]

In 2022, the share of external debt to GDP was 17%, decreasing from 26.3% in 2021.[^cite_note-239] Russia's external debt was estimated at 381.8 billion U.S. dollars as of 1 January 2023, down 20.8 percent from the previous year.[^cite_note-240] Russia External Debt reached US\$357.9 billion in March 2023, compared with US\$380.5 billion in the previous quarter.[^cite_note-241]

### National wealth fund

See also: [Russian National Wealth Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Wealth_Fund)

On 1 January 2004, the [Government of Russia](9787373.md) established the [Stabilization fund of the Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stabilization_fund_of_the_Russian_Federation) as part of the [federal budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_budget_of_Russia) to balance it if [price of oil](5137675.md) falls. On 1 February 2008, the Stabilization fund was divided into two parts. The first is a reserve fund equal to 10% of GDP and was to be invested in a similar way as the Stabilization Fund. The second is the National Welfare Fund of the Russian Federation to be invested in more risky instruments, including some shares in domestic and foreign companies. The Reserve fund which started with \$125 billion was exhausted by 2017 and discontinued. The National Wealth Fund had started with \$32 billion in 2008 and by August 2022, peaked at \$201 billion.[^cite_note-242] December 2023 saw it fall to \$133 billion with liquid assets also down at \$56 billion.[^cite_note-243]

### Corruption

Main article: [Corruption in Russia](27352290.md)



[Corruption Perceptions Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Perceptions_Index) by Transparency International, 2019

Russia was the lowest rated European country in [Transparency International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transparency_International)'s [Corruption Perceptions Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Perceptions_Index) for 2025; ranking 157th out of 181 countries.[^cite_note-244] Corruption is perceived as a significant problem in Russia,[^cite_note-CPI2015-245] affecting various aspects of life, including the economy,[^cite_note-246] business,[^cite_note-247] [public administration](9787373.md),[^cite_note-Suhara-248][^cite_note-249] [law enforcement](11137255.md),[^cite_note-250] [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Russia),[^cite_note-251] and [education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Russia).[^cite_note-252] The phenomenon of corruption is strongly established in the historical model of public governance in Russia and attributed to general weakness of [rule of law](25166191.md) in Russia.[^cite_note-253] As of 2020, the percentage of business owners who distrust law enforcement agencies rose to 70% (from 45% in 2017); 75% don't believe in impartiality of courts and 79% do not believe that legal institutions protect them from abuse of law such as racketeering or arrest on dubious grounds.[^cite_note-254]

## Income inequality

See also: [Income in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_in_Russia), [Gender pay gap in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_pay_gap_in_Russia), and [List of Russian federal subjects by average wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_federal_subjects_by_average_wage)

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. The reason given is: **cites are over 15 years old**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(January 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

In Russia, areas where income is higher have increased air pollution. However while income may have been higher in these regions a greater disparity in [income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_inequality) was found. It was discovered that "greater income inequality within a region is associated with more pollution, implying that it is not only the level of income that matters but also its distribution".[^cite_note-\:1a-255] In Russia areas lacking in hospital beds suffer from greater air pollution than areas with higher numbers of beds per capita which implies that the poor or inadequate distribution of public services also may add to the environmental inequality of that region.[^cite_note-\:1a-255]

## Regional economies and economic regions

### Federal subjects

Main articles: [List of Russian federal districts by GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_federal_districts_by_GDP), [List of federal subjects of Russia by GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_federal_subjects_of_Russia_by_GDP_per_capita), and [List of Russian federal subjects by GRDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_federal_subjects_by_GRDP)

According to the [Moscow Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_Government), the Moscow economy is the largest among the Russian Federation constituent entities: the capital accounts for one fifth of the country's total [gross regional product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_regional_product). Moscow is one of the largest urban economies in the world in terms of GDP ([purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity), PPP) and one of the top 5 global megalopolises according to this value.[^cite_note-auto11-256] The Moscow GDP (PPP) in 2021 amounted to US\$25.500 billion, which is more than that of Seoul, Shanghai or Paris.[^cite_note-auto11-256] The government refers to Oxford Economics stating that Moscow ranked third among the world's cities in terms of GDP at purchasing power parity in 2020.[^cite_note-257]

According to statements by the [Mayor of Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Moscow) in 2024, Moscow contributed more than 20 per cent of the country's GDP. This contribution is related to innovation, IT sector and services. [Labour productivity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_productivity) in Moscow is on average 2.5 times higher than in Russia due to the high concentration of residents.[^cite_note-258]

In May 2025, Mayor Sobyanin reported to President Putin that Moscow's gross regional product at purchasing power parity for 2024 had grown to [int\$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Int\$) 1.39 trillion, allowing the Russian capital to rank second among the world's largest urban economies, second only to New York and ahead of Shanghai. "In 2020, we were in fifth place and since 2023, we have moved up to second place," said the mayor of Moscow.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261]

Moscow is home to 90 [billionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billionaires) in 2025 – 16 more than a year ago. Moscow comes in second place behind New York City according to [Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes).[^cite_note-262]

### Groupings

Main articles: [Economic regions of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_regions_of_Russia) and [List of economic zones and macrozones of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_economic_zones_and_macrozones_of_Russia)

Russia is divided into [twelve economic regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_regions_of_Russia).[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264]

- [Central Black Earth Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Black_Earth_Economic_Region)

- [Central Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Economic_Region)

- [East Siberian Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Siberian_Economic_Region)

- [Far Eastern Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_Eastern_Economic_Region)

- [Kaliningrad Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaliningrad_Economic_Region)

- [North Caucasus Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Caucasus_Economic_Region)

- [Northern Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Economic_Region)

- [Northwestern Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Economic_Region)

- [Ural Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ural_Economic_Region)

- [Volga Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volga_Economic_Region)

- [Volga-Vyatka Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volga-Vyatka_Economic_Region)

- [West Siberian Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Siberian_Economic_Region)

In 2018, the [Russian Ministry of Economic Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Ministry_of_Economic_Development) developed a strategy for the spatial development of Russia, in which it was proposed to introduce the division into 14 macro-regions.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] In February 2019, the strategy was approved and the division into 12 macro-regions was implemented.[^cite_note-267]

There are also macrozones such as the [Baikal Amur Mainline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baikal_Amur_Mainline) economic zone.

### Centre-periphery model

Main article: [Core–periphery model in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Core%E2%80%93periphery_model_in_Russia)

According to a 2011 research article, the nature of the relationship between the centre and the regions in Russia today corresponds to the so-called "[centre-periphery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-periphery)" model; excessive centralisation, the absence of alternative (to Moscow) centres in the country's territorial structure and weak horizontal links are stable characteristics of the Russian political and economic space and are constantly reproduced over time.[^cite_note-268]

More than a decade ago, Professor [Natalia Zubarevich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalia_Zubarevich) proposed an extension of the centre-periphery model and is known as the author of the "theory of four Russias".[^cite_note-auto7-269] According to Zubarevich, the different speed of [social modernisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_modernisation) is more accurately explained by the centre-periphery model. The entire population of the country can be divided into three roughly equal parts - about a third of citizens in each. The [underdeveloped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underdeveloped) republics, where 6% of the country's population lives - this "fourth" Russia has its own specific features.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-auto6-271][^cite_note-272] According to the concept, "First Russia" is the [cities with millions of inhabitants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_with_over_one_million_inhabitants), i.e. the most modernised and economically developed territories. "Second Russia" are medium-sized cities with a pronounced industrial profile. "Third Russia" - small towns, workers' settlements, rural areas. Compared to "first" and "second Russia" - this is a deep periphery in terms of the quality of socio-economic life. The "fourth Russia" is made up of the national republics of the Caucasus, as well as the south of Siberia (Tuva, the Altai Republic). These territories also represent a periphery, but a specific one: the [demographic transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_transition) has not been completed here, [urbanisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanisation) is in its infancy, and [patriarchal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarchal)-[clan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clan) principles are still strong in society.[^cite_note-auto7-269]

Monoprofile towns ([monotowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monotown)) are the most unstable part of the "second Russia".[^cite_note-auto6-271]

## Sectors

### Primary

#### Energy

Main article: [Energy in Russia](14049009.md)

See also: [Renewable energy in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Russia), [Energy policy of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_policy_of_Russia), [Electricity sector in Russia](31006441.md), [Petroleum industry in Russia](10450931.md), [Natural gas in Russia](42827258.md), [Oil reserves in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves_in_Russia), and [Russia in the European energy sector](24648652.md)



Until 2022, Russia was a key [oil](23195.md) and [gas](22131.md) supplier to much of Europe.



[Rosneft](1313566.md) headquarters on the bank of the [Moskva River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moskva_River), Moscow

The mineral-packed [Ural Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ural_Mountains) and the vast [fossil fuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel) (oil, gas, [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_in_Russia)), and timber reserves of [Siberia](29639.md) and the [Russian Far East](173135.md) make Russia rich in natural resources, which dominate Russian exports. Oil and gas exports, specifically, continue to be the main source of [hard currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_currency).

Russia has been widely described as an [energy superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_superpower);[^cite_note-\:0-273] as it has the [world's largest natural gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_proven_reserves),[^cite_note-gaz-34] the [second-largest coal reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_coal_reserves),[^cite_note-\:2-35] the [eighth-largest oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves),[^cite_note-\:3-36] and the largest [oil shale reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_shale_reserves) in Europe.[^cite_note-\:4-37] It is the [second-largest natural gas producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_production)[^cite_note-\:6-39] and [natural gas exporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_exports), the [second-largest oil exporter](17808102.md)[^cite_note-\:7-40] and [oil producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_oil_production),[^cite_note-\:8-41] and the third largest coal exporter.[^cite_note-Energy_Research_2023-42] Fossil fuels cause most of the [greenhouse gas emissions by Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions_by_Russia).[^cite_note-274] The country was the world's fourth-largest [electricity producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_producer),[^cite_note-275] and the ninth-largest [renewable energy producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_producer) in 2019.[^cite_note-276] Russia was also the world's first country to develop civilian nuclear power, and built the world's [first nuclear power plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obninsk_Nuclear_Power_Plant).[^cite_note-277] In 2019, It was the world's fourth-largest [nuclear energy producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_by_country).[^cite_note-278] After the collapse of the Soviet Union, the Russian state nuclear conglomerate Rosatom became the dominant actor in international nuclear power markets, training experts, constructing nuclear power plants, supplying fuel and taking care of spent fuel in around the world.[^cite_note-\:10-279] Whereas oil and gas were subject to international sanctions after Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2023, its nuclear industry was not targeted by sanctions.[^cite_note-\:10-279]

In the mid-2000s, the share of the oil and gas sector in GDP was around 20%, and in 2013 it was 20–21% of GDP.[^cite_note-280] The share of oil and gas in Russia's exports (about 50%) and federal budget revenues (about 50%) is large, and the dynamics of Russia's GDP are highly dependent on oil and gas prices,[^cite_note-281] but the share in GDP is much less than 50%. According to the first such comprehensive assessment published by the Russian statistics agency Rosstat in 2021, the maximum total share of the oil and gas sector in Russia's GDP, including extraction, refining, transport, sale of oil and gas, all goods and services used, and all supporting activities, amounted to 16.9% in 2017, 21.1% in 2018, 19.2% in 2019 and 15.2% in 2020. To compare the data obtained using the same methodology, the source provides data for other countries. This is more than the share of GDP in the United States (8%) and Canada (less 10%). This is comparable to the share of GDP in Norway (14%) and Kazakhstan (13.3%). It is much lower than the share of GDP in the United Arab Emirates (30%) and Saudi Arabia (50%). This assessment did not include, for example, the production of used pumps or specialized education, which should have been included, according to experts.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286] Russia participates in the [OPEC+](166346.md) [cartel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartel).[^cite_note-g417-287]

Russia consumes domestically two-thirds of its gas production and a quarter of its oil production while it sells three-quarters of its oil on the world market and Russia's share of the traded world oil market is 17.5% - more than Saudi Arabia's.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] At the same time, experts note that there are formal and informal part of the rent and the total oil and gas rent in 2023 can be estimated at 24% of Russia's GDP. Michael Alexeyev (son of [Lyudmila Alexeyeva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyudmila_Alexeyeva)), a professor of economics at [Indiana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University), notes that the oil and gas taxes reported by the government do not include corporate dividends and the so-called indirect or additional revenues derived from the expenditure of oil and gas rents in the economy.[^cite_note-290] There is also such an indicator as the oil rent (% of GDP), which is published by the World Bank. It is 9.7% for Russia, 14.8% for Kazakhstan, 6.1% for Norway, 23.7% for Saudi Arabia, 15.7% for the United Arab Emirates, 2.8% for Canada and 0.6% for the United States.[^cite_note-291]

2023 saw a fall in Russia's oil and gas tax revenues of 24% to 8.8 trillion roubles (\$99.4 billion) compared to 2022.[^cite_note-292]

As of September 2025, Ukrainian drone and missile strikes have disrupted nearly 40% of Russia's oil refining capacity, forcing Moscow to import gasoline from Asian nations and creating widespread fuel shortages across multiple Russian regions. The sustained campaign has knocked approximately 338,000 tons per day of crude oil processing offline, with over 20 regions experiencing shortages and gas stations implementing fuel rationing measures.[^cite_note-newsweek-fuel-shortage-293]

In September 2025 the [International Energy Agency](285292.md) stated that Russia's revenues from [oil product exports](10450931.md) had in August declined to five-year lows, contributing to Russia's economic slowdown.[^cite_note-294]

#### Mining

Main article: [Mining industry of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Russia)

See also: [List of mines in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mines_in_Russia), [Metallurgy of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metallurgy_of_Russia), and [Coal in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_in_Russia)

Russia is also a leading producer and exporter of minerals and gold. Russia is the largest diamond-producing country in the world, estimated to produce over 33 million [carats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carat_\(mass\)) in 2013, or 25% of global output valued at over \$3.4 billion, with state-owned [ALROSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ALROSA) accounting for approximately 95% of all Russian production.[^cite_note-Kitco-295]

In 2019, the country was the 3rd world producer of [gold](12240.md);[^cite_note-296] 2nd worldwide producer of [platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum);[^cite_note-297] 4th worldwide producer of [silver](27119.md);[^cite_note-298] 9th largest world producer of [copper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper);[^cite_note-299] 3rd largest world producer of [nickel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickel);[^cite_note-300] 6th largest world producer of [lead](17747.md);[^cite_note-301] 9th largest world producer of [bauxite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauxite);[^cite_note-302] 10th largest world producer of [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc);[^cite_note-303] 2nd worldwide producer of [vanadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanadium);[^cite_note-304] 2nd largest world producer of [cobalt](24580536.md);[^cite_note-305] 5th largest world producer of [iron ore](145352.md);[^cite_note-306] 7th largest world producer of [boron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boron);[^cite_note-307] 9th largest world producer of [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum);[^cite_note-308] 13th largest world producer of [tin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tin);[^cite_note-309] 3rd largest world producer of [sulfur](27127.md);[^cite_note-310] 4th largest world producer of [phosphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phosphate);[^cite_note-311] 8th largest world producer of [gypsum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gypsum);[^cite_note-312] in addition to being the world's 10th largest producer of [salt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_salt).[^cite_note-313] It was the world's 6th largest producer of [uranium](31743.md) in 2018.[^cite_note-314]

#### Agriculture, fishing and forestry

Main articles: [Agriculture in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Russia), [Fishing industry in Russia](20919132.md), and [Forestry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forestry_in_Russia)

See also: [Russian grain exports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_grain_exports) and [Tractor, timber and agricultural machinery in the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tractor,_timber_and_agricultural_machinery_in_the_Soviet_Union)



A [combine harvester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combine_harvester) in [Rostov Oblast](478744.md)

Russia's agriculture sector contributes about 5% of the country's total GDP, although the sector employs about one-eighth of the total labour force.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-315] It has the world's [third-largest cultivated area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_use_statistics_by_country), at 1,265,267 square kilometres (488,522 sq mi). However, due to the harshness of its environment, about 13.1% of its land is [agricultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_land),[^cite_note-CIAWFRS-1] and only 7.4% of its land is [arable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_land).[^cite_note-316] The main product of Russian farming has always been [grain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grain), which occupies considerably more than half of the cropland.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-315] Russia is the world's [largest exporter of wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_wheat_exports),[^cite_note-317] and is the largest producer of [barley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barley),[^cite_note-318] [buckwheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckwheat), [oats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oats),[^cite_note-319] and [rye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rye),[^cite_note-320] and the second-largest producer of [sunflower seed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunflower_seed).[^cite_note-321] Various analysts of [climate change adaptation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_adaptation) foresee large opportunities for Russian agriculture during the rest of the 21st century as arability increases in Siberia, which would lead to both internal and external migration to the region.[^cite_note-climatechange-322]

More than one-third of the sown area is devoted to fodder crops, and the remaining farmland is devoted to [industrial crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonfood_crop), vegetables, and fruits.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-315] Owing to its large coastline along three oceans, Russia maintains one of the world's [largest fishing fleets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing_industry_by_country), ranking sixth in the world in tonnage of fish caught; capturing 4.77 million tonnes (4.69 million long tons; 5.26 million short tons) of fish in 2018.[^cite_note-323] It is also home to the world's finest caviar (the [beluga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beluga_\(sturgeon\))), and produces about one-third of all canned fish, and some one-fourth of the world's total fresh and frozen fish.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-315]

### Industry



Russia's [industrial growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_industrial_production_growth_rate) per year (%), 1992–2010

#### Defense

Main article: [Defense industry of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry_of_Russia)

The [defense industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry) of Russia is a strategically important sector and a large employer in the country. Russia has a large and sophisticated arms industry, capable of designing and manufacturing high-tech military equipment, including a [fifth-generation fighter jet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_PAK_FA), [nuclear powered submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_submarine), [firearms](11966.md), and [short range/long range ballistic missiles](38025.md).[^cite_note-CIAWFRS-1]

  A view of [T-14 Armata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-14_Armata) tank

#### Aerospace



The [Sukhoi Superjet 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Superjet_100) is one of Russia's most recent civilian aviation products. The regional passenger plane was ordered around 280 times for various airlines and leasing companies as of 2018.

Main articles: [Aircraft industry of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aircraft_industry_of_Russia) and [Space industry of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_industry_of_Russia)

Aircraft manufacturing is an important industry sector in Russia, employing around 355,300 people. The Russian aircraft industry offers a portfolio of internationally competitive military aircraft such as [MiG-29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-29) and [Su-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-30), while new projects such as the [Sukhoi Superjet 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Superjet_100) are hoped to revive the fortunes of the civilian aircraft segment. In 2009, companies belonging to the [United Aircraft Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Aircraft_Corporation) delivered 95 new fixed-wing aircraft to its customers, including 15 civilian models. In addition, the industry produced over 141 helicopters. It is one of the most science-intensive hi-tech sectors and employs the largest number of skilled personnel. The production and value of the military aircraft branch far outstrips other defence industry sectors, and aircraft products make up more than half of the country's arms exports.[^cite_note-manturov-324]

The [Space industry of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_industry_of_Russia) consists of over 100 companies and employs 250,000 people.[^cite_note-mdb-325]

#### Automotive

Main article: [Automotive industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Russia)

See also: [List of automobile manufacturers of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_automobile_manufacturers_of_Russia)



[Lada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lada) is a brand of [AvtoVAZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AvtoVAZ), the largest Russian car manufacturer in the [Russian automotive industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_automotive_industry).



[Aurus Senat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurus_Senat), a recent [armoured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_vehicle) [limousine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limousine) project by [NAMI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NAMI_\(automotive_institute\))

Following the 2022 sanctions and the withdrawal of Western manufacturers the production dropped to 450,000 passenger cars in 2022, the lowest level since the dissolution of the Soviet Union in 1991.[^cite_note-326] The main local brands are light vehicle producers [AvtoVAZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AvtoVAZ) and [GAZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GAZ), while [KamAZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamaz) is the leading heavy vehicle producer. In December 2022 the only foreign car manufacturers are eleven Chinese carmakers that have production operations or are constructing their plants in Russia.[^cite_note-327]

#### Chemical

Main article: [Chemical industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_industry_in_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Chemical industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_industry_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chemical_industry_in_Russia\&action=edit)]

[The Russian chemical industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_industry_in_Russia) is a branch of [Russian industry](#Industry).

The chemical industry's share of Russia's GDP in 2006 was about 6% of exports.

In 2009 it exported 3.1 million tons of ammonia in the amount of 626 million dollars, 814 thousand tons of [methanol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methanol) in the amount of 156 million dollars, 22 million tons of mineral fertilizers worth \$5.6 billion, 702 thousand tons of [synthetic rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthetic_rubber) in the amount of 1, \$2 billion.[^cite_note-Chemical_industry_in_Russia_strukteksprf-328]

The average monthly wage in the chemical industry in March 2010 was 21956 RUR / month.[^cite_note-ugn0410-329] The Russian chemical industry was facing problems in 2014 due to the sanctions imposed on Russia by Western nations.[^cite_note-330]

Russia was experiencing a regrowth of microelectronics, with the revival of [JCS Mikron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikron_Group) until sanctions took effect in 2022.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

#### Food manufacturing

Main article: [Food industry of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_industry_of_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Food industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_industry_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Food_industry_in_Russia\&action=edit)]



Wheat harvesting in Rostov Oblast

The [food industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_industry_in_Russia) is a significant sector in the economy of Russia.

The volume of production in the manufacture of food products and tobacco - 3.12 trillion rubles (in 2010), including:[^cite_note-Food_industry_in_Russia_rsvip-334]

- Food production - 2.952 trillion rubles;

- Manufacture of tobacco products - 164 billion rubles.

As of 2020, Russia faces problems of over-nutrition with over 23% of the adults obese and over 57% overweight. Under 2.5% of the population suffer from undernourishment. Russia has formulated a Development of Healthy Lifestyle policy covering the years 2017 to 2025.[^cite_note-Food_industry_in_Russia_RuJE-2020-v6-n1-335]

#### Pharmaceutical

Main article: [Pharmaceutical industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Pharmaceutical industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia\&action=edit)]

[The pharmaceutical industry in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia) had a turnover of \$16.5 billion in 2016, which was equal to 1.3 % of GDP and 19.9% of health spending.[^cite_note-Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia_expgov-336]

The Russian healthcare system is funded by the federal government, social spending and the [Federal Compulsory Medical Insurance Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Compulsory_Medical_Insurance_Fund_\(Russia\)). However, the industry expects a big shift towards the private healthcare spending.[^cite_note-Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia_expgov-336]

Russia is a net importer of pharmaceuticals, importing \$8.9 billion while exporting \$635 million of medicines. Close to 80% of imports come from Europe, mostly from Germany and France.[^cite_note-Pharmaceutical_industry_in_Russia_rupharmdel-337]

[Shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipbuilding) is an established industry in [Russia](25391.md) and is considered strategically important to both the country's [armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_forces) and national economy.[^cite_note-Shipbuilding_in_Russia_\:0-338]

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Science and technology in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Science_and_technology_in_Russia\&action=edit)]



[Shukhov Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shukhov_Tower) in Moscow

[Science and technology in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Russia) have developed rapidly since the [Age of Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment), when [Peter the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_the_Great) founded the [Russian Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Academy_of_Sciences) and [Saint Petersburg State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg_State_University) and [polymath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polymath) [Mikhail Lomonosov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Lomonosov) founded the [Moscow State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_State_University), establishing a strong native tradition in learning and innovation.

In the 19th and 20th centuries, [Russia](25391.md) produced many notable [scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_scientists), making important contributions in [physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physics), [astronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astronomy), [mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathematics), [computing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_sciences), [chemistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemistry), [biology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biology), [geology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology) and [geography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography). [Russian inventors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_inventors) and engineers excelled in such areas as [electrical engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_engineering), [shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipbuilding), [aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace), [weaponry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weaponry), [communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications), [IT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology), [nuclear technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_technology) and [space technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_technology).

The crisis of the 1990s led to the drastic reduction of state support for science and technology, leading many Russian scientists and university graduates to move to Western Europe or the United States. In the 2000s, on the wave of a new economic boom, the situation has improved, and the Russian government [launched a campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medvedev_modernisation_programme) aimed into [modernisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernisation) and [innovation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innovation) with mixed success.

### Services

#### Banking and finance

Main articles: [Banking in Russia](9608840.md), [Stock market in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_market_in_Russia), and [List of banks in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Russia)

#### Education

Main article: [Education in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Education in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Education_in_Russia\&action=edit)]

In [Russia](25391.md), the state provides most education services regulating education through the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(Russia\)) and the [Ministry of Science and Higher Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Science_and_Higher_Education_\(Russia\)). Regional authorities regulate education within their jurisdictions within the prevailing framework of federal laws. Russia's expenditure on education has grown from 2.7% of the [GDP](12594.md) in 2005 to 4.7% in 2018 but remains below the [OECD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) average of 4.9%.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340]

Before 1990 the course of school training in the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) lasted 10 years, but at the end of 1990, an 11-year course officially came into operation. Education in state-owned secondary schools is free; *first* tertiary (university level) education is free with reservations: a substantial number of students enrol on full pay. Male and female students have equal shares in all stages of education,[^cite_note-Education_in_Russia_EFA82-341] except in [tertiary education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_education) where women lead with 57%.[^cite_note-Education_in_Russia_EFA316-342]

A 2015 estimate by the United States [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) puts the [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_rate) in [Russia](25391.md) at 99.7% (99.7% for men, 99.6% for women).[^cite_note-343] According to a 2016 [OECD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) estimate, 54% of Russia's adults (25- to 64-year-olds) have attained tertiary education, giving Russia the second-highest attainment of tertiary education among 35 OECD member countries.[^cite_note-Education_in_Russia_keepeek.com-344] 47.7% have completed [secondary education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_education) (the full 11-year course); 26.5% have completed [middle school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_school) (9 years) and 8.1% have [primary education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_education) (at least 4 years). The highest rates of [tertiary education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_education) (24.7%) are recorded among women aged 35 to 39 years (compared to 19.5% for men of the same age bracket).[^cite_note-345]

Compared with other OECD countries, Russia has close to average class sizes[^cite_note-346] and some of the shortest instruction hours per year.[^cite_note-347]

In 2014 the Pearson/[Economist Intelligence Unit](15303116.md) rated Russia's education as the 8th-best in Europe and the 13th-best in the world;[^cite_note-348] Russia's educational attainment was rated as the 21st-highest in the world, and the students' cognitive skills as the 9th-highest.[^cite_note-349]

In 2015 the OECD ranked Russian students' mathematics and science skills as the 34th-best in the world, between [Sweden](5058739.md) and [Iceland](14531.md).[^cite_note-350]

In 2016 the US company [Bloomberg](432006.md) rated Russia's higher education as the third-best in the world, measuring the percentage of high-school graduates who go on to attend college, the annual science and engineering graduates as a percentage of all college graduates, and science and engineering graduates as a percentage of the labor force.[^cite_note-351]

In 2014 Russia ranked as the 6th most popular destination for international students.[^cite_note-352]

The Human Rights Measurement Initiative[^cite_note-353] finds that Russia is fulfilling 86.8% of what it should be fulfilling for the right to education, based on its level of income.[^cite_note-354]

In 2025, Russia continued to maintain a large higher-education system, with more than 1,200 universities and over 619,000 state-funded higher-education places available for new students. The Russian Ministry of Science and Higher Education also reported that more than 395,000 international students were studying at Russian universities within the country, with the government aiming to increase the number of international students to at least 500,000 by 2030.

#### Healthcare

Main article: [Healthcare in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Healthcare in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Healthcare_in_Russia\&action=edit)]



Ambulance car-based [GAZelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GAZelle) is the most common type of ambulance in Russia

Healthcare in [Russia](25391.md) is provided by the state through the [Federal Compulsory Medical Insurance Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Compulsory_Medical_Insurance_Fund_\(Russia\)), and regulated through the [Ministry of Health](29896200.md).[^cite_note-Healthcare_in_Russia_popovich-355] The [Constitution of the Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Russia) has provided all [citizens](17005363.md) the right to free [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care) since 1993. In 2008, 621,000 doctors and 1.3 million nurses were employed in Russian healthcare. The number of doctors per 10,000 people was 43.8, but only 12.1 in rural areas. The number of general practitioners as a share of the total number of doctors was 1.26 percent. There are about 9.3 beds per thousand population—nearly double the [OECD](33853117.md) average.

Expenditure on healthcare was 6.5% of [Gross domestic product](12594.md), US\$957 per person in 2013. About 48% comes from government sources which primarily come from medical insurance deductions from salaries. About 5% of the population, mostly in major cities, have voluntary health insurance.[^cite_note-Healthcare_in_Russia_britnell2015-356]

The total [population of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Russia) in 2016 was 146.8 million. Among this population, the number of employed individuals reached 72.3 million, involved in the 99 main types of productive and nonproductive activities.[^cite_note-357] In modern conditions, the world experts estimate the overall [health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health) of the Russian working population (men 18–60 years, women 18–55 years) to be rather low due to the high [mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortality_rate), significantly higher male mortality level, and a high [prevalence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prevalence) of major [noncommunicable diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-communicable_disease), especially those of the [circulatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circulatory_system), [respiratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_system), and [digestive system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_digestive_system). According to official government statistics, 1 of every 3 workers in [Russia](25391.md) is exposed to [harmful working conditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_safety_and_health) in which the levels of exposure in the workplace exceed the national hygienic standards. However, the level of [occupational morbidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_fatality) in [Russia](25391.md) remains extremely low. In 2014, only 8175 cases of [occupational diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_disease) were reported, representing 5.5 cases per 100,000 in the general population, a rate much less than in many European countries.[^cite_note-Healthcare_in_Russia_Mazitova_et_al_2015-358]

After the end of the Soviet Union, Russian healthcare became composed of state and private systems. Drastic cuts in funding to the state-run healthcare system brought declines in the quality of healthcare it provided. This made pricier private facilities competitive by marketing themselves as providing better-quality healthcare. After [Boris Yeltsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Yeltsin) resigned, privatization was no longer the priority, with [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) bringing back higher funding to the state-owned healthcare system. The state healthcare system greatly improved throughout the 2000s, with health spending per person rising from \$96 in 2000 to \$957 in 2013.

Due to the [Russian financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_financial_crisis_\(2014%E2%80%932016\)) since 2014, major cuts in health spending have resulted in a decline in the quality of service of the state healthcare system. About 40% of basic medical facilities have fewer staff than they are supposed to have, with others being closed down. Waiting periods for treatment have increased, and patients have been forced to pay for more services that were previously free.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]

#### Retail

See also: [Grocery retailing in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grocery_retailing_in_Russia), [List of supermarket chains in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_supermarket_chains_in_Russia), and [Category\:Russian brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Russian_brands)

As of 2013, Russians spent 60% of their pre-tax income on shopping, the highest percentage in Europe. This is possible because many Russians pay no rent or house payments, owning their own home after privatization of state-owned Soviet housing. [Shopping malls](235723.md) were popular with international investors and shoppers from the emerging middle class. Russia had over 1,000 shopping malls in 2020, although in 2022, many international companies left Russia resulting in empty stores in malls.[^cite_note-361] A [supermarket](51862.md) selling groceries is a typical anchor store in a Russian mall.[^cite_note-NYT010113-362]

#### Telecommunications

Main articles: [Telecommunications in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Russia) and [Internet in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_in_Russia)

In 2024 the revenue of Russian telecom operators amounted to more than 2.1 trillion rubles, which was 7.8% more than the previous year.[^cite_note-auto9-364] There are four national mobile phone networks, MegaFon, Tele2, Beeline and MTS with total subscriptions between 2011 and 2021 ranging between 200 and 240 million.[^cite_note-365] Although almost everyone uses the internet, as of 2026 [Telegram](41501305.md) is throttled and [WhatsApp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WhatsApp) is blocked.[^cite_note-\:5-44] As of 2024, Russia has 270 million active mobile subscribers, 188 million of them are mobile Internet users and 39 million active fixed-line Internet subscribers.[^cite_note-auto9-364]

#### Transportation

Main article: [Transport in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Russia)

See also: [List of motorways in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_motorways_in_Russia), [Rail transport in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Russia), [List of airports in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Russia), and [Category\:Ports and harbours of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Ports_and_harbours_of_Russia)



The [Trans-Siberian Railway](56912.md), the longest railway-line in the world, as seen across the coast of [Lake Baikal](56865.md)



The [Russky Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russky_Bridge) in [Vladivostok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladivostok) is the longest cable-stayed bridge in the world.

[Railway transport in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Russia) is mostly under the control of the state-run [Russian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Railways).[^cite_note-366] The total length of common-used railway tracks is the world's [third-longest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_rail_transport_network_size), and exceeds 87,157 km (54,157 mi).[^cite_note-367] As of 2016, Russia has [1,452.2 thousand km of roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_by_country),[^cite_note-368] and its road density is among the world's lowest.[^cite_note-369] Russia's inland waterways are the world's [second-longest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_waterways_length), and total 102,000 km (63,380 mi).[^cite_note-370] Among [Russia's 1,218 airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Russia),[^cite_note-371] the [busiest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_Russia) is [Sheremetyevo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheremetyevo_International_Airport) in Moscow.[^cite_note-372] As of 2026 air transport is hampered by sanctions on spare parts from Boeing and Airbus, and by drones disrupting airports.[^cite_note-\:5-44]



The [Port of Novorossiysk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Novorossiysk)

Russia is the world's sole country to operate [nuclear-powered icebreakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear-powered_icebreaker), which advance the economic exploitation of the Arctic [continental shelf of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_shelf_of_Russia), and the development of sea trade through the [Northern Sea Route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Sea_Route).[^cite_note-373]

#### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Russia)

This section is an excerpt from [Water supply and sanitation in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Russia).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Russia\&action=edit)]

In [Russia](25391.md), approximately 70 per cent of drinking water comes from surface water and 30 per cent from groundwater. In 2004, water supply systems had a total capacity of 90 million cubic metres a day. The average residential water use was 248 litres per capita per day.[^cite_note-Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Russia_UN-374] One quarter of the world's fresh surface and [groundwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundwater) is located in Russia. The water utilities sector is one of the largest industries in Russia serving the entire Russian population.

#### Construction and real estate

Main article: [Housing in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_Russia)

In 2022, construction was worth 13 trillion rubles, 5% more than in 2021. Residential construction in 2022 reached 126.7 million square metres (1,364 million square feet).[^cite_note-mos218-375]

The 2020–2030 target for construction is 1 billion square metres (1.1×10<sup>10</sup> square feet) of housing, 20% of all housing stock to be renovated and to increase space from 27.8 square metres (299 sq ft) up to 33.3 square metres (358 sq ft) per person.[^cite_note-mos218-375]

#### Insurance

See also: [Pension and Social Insurance Fund of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pension_and_Social_Insurance_Fund_of_Russia)

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(October 2025)* |
| --- | --- |

According to the Central Bank of Russia 422 insurance companies operate on the Russian insurance market by the end of 2013. The concentration of insurance business is significant across all major segments except [compulsory motor third party liability market (CMTPL)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_motor_third_party_liability_market_\(CMTPL\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), as the top 10 companies in 2013 charged 58.1% premiums in total without compulsory health insurance (CHI).[^cite_note-376]

Although relative indicators of the Russian insurance market returned to pre-crisis levels, the progress is achieved mainly by the increase of life insurance and accident insurance, the input of these two market segments in premium growth in 2013 largely exceeds their share on the market. As before, life insurance and accident insurance are often used by banks as an appendix to a credit contract protecting creditors from the risk of credit default in case of borrower's death or disability. The rise of these lines is connected, evidently, with the increase in consumer loans, as the total sum of credit obligations of population in 2013 increased by 28% to RUB 9.9 trillion. At the same time premium to GDP ratio net of life and accident insurance remained at the same level of 1.1% as in 2012. Thus, if "banking" lines of business are excluded, Russian insurance market is in stagnation stage for the last four years, as premiums to GDP ratio net of life and accident insurance remains at the same level of 1.1% since 2010.[^cite_note-377]

#### Information technology

Main article: [Information technology in Russia](29175889.md)



Russia has more academic graduates than any other country in Europe (and world leader in percentage of population with associate degree or higher: 54%, compared to 31% in UK

[^cite_note-378]

).

Russian software exports have risen from just \$120 million in 2000 to \$3.3 billion in 2010.[^cite_note-Russoft-379] Since the year 2000 the IT market has started growth rates of 30–40% a year, growing by 54% in 2006 alone. The biggest sector in terms of revenue is system and network integration, which accounts for 28.3% of the total market revenues.[^cite_note-380]



Interior of the Beriev Scientific and Technical Complex in [Taganrog](1288065.md)

The government has launched a program promoting construction of IT-oriented technology parks (Technoparks)—special zones that have an established infrastructure and enjoy a favorable tax and customs regime, in seven different locations: Moscow, Novosibirsk, Nizhny Novgorod, Kaluga, Tumen, Republic of Tatarstan and St. Peterburg Region.[^cite_note-Russoft-379]

Under a [government decree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_the_Government_of_Russia) signed in June 2013, a special "roadmap" is expected to ease business suppliers' access to the procurement programs of state-owned infrastructure monopolies, including such large ones as [Gazprom](320489.md), [Rosneft](1313566.md), [Russian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Railways), [Rosatom](1641369.md), and [Transneft](5707669.md). These companies will be expected to increase the proportion of domestic technology solutions they use in their operations. The decree puts special emphasis on purchases of innovation products and technologies. According to the new decree, by 2015, government-connected companies must double their purchases of Russian technology solutions compared to the 2013 level and their purchasing levels must quadruple by 2018.[^cite_note-381]

Russia is one of the few countries in the world with a homegrown [internet search engine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_search_engine) with a significant marketshare as the Russian-based search engine [Yandex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yandex) is used by 53.8% of internet users in the country.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]

Known Russian IT companies are [ABBYY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABBYY) ([FineReader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FineReader) OCR system and Lingvo dictionaries), [Kaspersky Lab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaspersky_Lab) ([Kaspersky Anti-Virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaspersky_Anti-Virus), [Kaspersky Internet Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaspersky_Internet_Security)), [Mail.Ru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mail.Ru) (portal, search engine, mail service, Mail.ru Agent messenger, [ICQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICQ), [Odnoklassniki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odnoklassniki) social network, online media sources).

#### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Russia](159643.md)



[Peterhof Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterhof_Palace) in Saint Petersburg, a [UNESCO](21786641.md) [World Heritage Site](44940.md)

Most foreign tourists come from China.[^cite_note-385] Major tourist routes in Russia include a journey around the [Golden Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Ring_\(Russia\)) [theme route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theme_route) of ancient cities, cruises on the big rivers like the [Volga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volga), and journeys on the famous [Trans-Siberian Railway](56912.md).[^cite_note-386] Russia's most visited and popular landmarks include [Red Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Square), the [Peterhof Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterhof_Palace), the [Kazan Kremlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazan_Kremlin), the [Trinity Lavra of St. Sergius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_Lavra_of_St._Sergius) and [Lake Baikal](56865.md).[^cite_note-387]

## Economic integration

|  |
| --- |
| Top 4 largest economies (China, the US, India, Russia) in the world by [PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity)-adjusted GDP in 2023 according to the [World Bank](36752.md), the members of the [Eurasian Economic Union](27290106.md) as well as [Tajikistan](30108.md) and [Uzbekistan](31853.md) that are forming a [common market within the CIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Economic_Space_of_the_Commonwealth_of_Independent_States) (EAEU+2)[^cite_note-388] |

Russia joined the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) (WTO) on 22 August 2012.[^cite_note-auto5-127] On 20 September 2012, the [Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States_Free_Trade_Area) of the Commonwealth of Independent States signed on 18 October 2011 came into force (CIS FTA) for Russia and superseded previous agreements.[^cite_note-auto2-130] Russia is a founding member of the [Eurasian Economic Union](27290106.md) (EAEU) and is party to EAEU trade agreements with Vietnam, Iran, Singapore, and Serbia. In 2018, the EAEU signed a trade cooperation agreement with China, and it is in trade negotiations with India, Israel, and Egypt. Russia is also a party to several agreements that predate the EAEU.[^cite_note-auto4-389] The EAEU Treaty in 2015 superseded previous integration agreements and envisaged an [economic union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_union) (the deepest stage of [economic integration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_integration)[^cite_note-auto3-131]). The EAEU provides for free movement of goods, services, capital and labour without a work permit ("[four economic freedoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_economic_freedoms)" as in the European Union), pursues coordinated, harmonized and single policy in the sectors determined by the Treaty and international agreements within the Union. The EAEU has a [Eurasian Customs Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Customs_Union) and an integrated [single market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single_market) of 183 million people.[^cite_note-auto-132]

## External trade and investment



Over the past 30 years, the share of imports of goods and services as a share of Russia's GDP has been below 25% for almost all years. The growth of oil rents, rapid economic growth, economic integration, WTO accession, improved transport accessibility and the government's import substitution program hardly changed the percentage, and only sanctions and boycotts in 2022 led to a drop to 15.6%.

[^cite_note-390]

### Foreign payments

Since the [collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nixon_shock) of the [Bretton Woods system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bretton_Woods_system) and the creation of the [Jamaican monetary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaica_Accords), membership in the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) no longer requires the use of the US dollar, and by 1973 the US dollar was no longer the sole [global currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_currency). Russia joined the IMF in 1992, although the USSR had previously refused to ratify the founding document.

The Soviet Union and other socialist countries used the so-called [transferable ruble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transferable_ruble?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [clearing ruble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clearing_ruble?action=edit\&redlink=1).

The [Interstate Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Bank) became the first international financial organization created by the Commonwealth of Independent States, which was given the specific goal of developing mutual settlements in national currencies. The mechanism for cross-border settlements in national currencies (payment and settlement system), based on the use of direct correspondent accounts with central banks and access to payment systems, has been in operation for more than 15 years (since circa 2005), as reported in 2020.[^cite_note-391] The Agreement on Establishment of the [Interstate Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Bank) was signed on 22 January 1993 by Armenia, Belarus, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Moldova, Russia, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan, Uzbekistan, Ukraine and ratified by all countries except Ukraine, which revoked its signature in 1997.[^cite_note-392]

Back in February 2010, online retailer [Sportmaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportmaster) announced its intention to become the first Russian company to switch to the national currency for settlements with suppliers and to use the yuan for trade with China.[^cite_note-393] On 13 December 2010, Bank [HSBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC) announced that it had processed Russia's first international transaction in yuan for the Sportmaster group of companies.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] As part of China's efforts to [internationalize the yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internationalization_of_the_renminbi), trading in the ruble/yuan currency pair began on the MICEX on 16 December 2010. The [Moscow Interbank Currency Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_Interbank_Currency_Exchange) (MICEX) became the first organized (i.e., exchange-based) platform for yuan trading outside China. This was made possible by an agreement signed in April 2010 on the mutual launch of trading in yuan and rubles. Since the end of November 2010, rubles can be bought in China on the [China Foreign Exchange Trade System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Foreign_Exchange_Trade_System).[^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397]

In 2017, a [payment versus payment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payment_versus_payment) (PVP) system was created for the first time for transactions between the yuan and foreign currencies, according to a statement published on the website of the China Foreign Exchange Trade System (CFETS). The ruble became the first foreign currency to be included in the system. Earlier, the Russian central bank opened its first overseas office in Beijing, and the [Industrial and Commercial Bank of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_and_Commercial_Bank_of_China) (ICBC) officially began operating as a Chinese clearing bank for yuan settlements in Russia.[^cite_note-398] In 2020, VTB's Shanghai branch connected to China's national domestic payment system, CNAPS ([China National Advanced Payment System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_National_Advanced_Payment_System)).[^cite_note-399] It has direct access to the CNAPS national payment system, which allows payments to be made to all local banks connected to this system, i.e., virtually all banks in China. Settlements are made through accounts with the [central bank of the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Bank_of_China).[^cite_note-400]

By 2022, 23 Russian banks had been connected to the Chinese [Cross-Border Interbank Payment System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-Border_Interbank_Payment_System). [^cite_note-401] Back in 2022, nine Russian banks, including Sberbank and VTB Bank, opened special accounts in India for settlements in rupees for Indian-Russian trade transactions in order to [internationalize the rupee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internationalize_the_rupee?action=edit\&redlink=1) and facilitate international trade operations.[^cite_note-402]

In 2023, the vice president of the Interstate Bank noted that payments in national currencies had increased significantly.[^cite_note-403] By 2023, the Russian ruble had become the main currency for trade with the CIS.[^cite_note-404]

In 2023, Elvira Nabiullina, Governor of the Bank of Russia, attended the [Asian Clearing Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Clearing_Union) summit in Tehran.[^cite_note-405] In 2023, the [Central Bank of Iran](3961876.md) announced that by connecting to the Russian Central Bank's payment system, it had also gained access to more than 100 banks in 13 countries, in addition to Russia.[^cite_note-406] An Iranian banking official revealed that all Russian banks and 106 banks in 13 other countries have been connected to Iran's financial messaging system known as [SEPAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEPAM?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-407] In 2024, Mohammad Reza Farzin, head of the Central Bank of Iran, announced a new [ACUMER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACUMER?action=edit\&redlink=1) system to replace SWIFT and a new agreement with Russia.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409] In 2024, Farzin emphasized Iran's readiness to provide technical capabilities for Russia's membership in the Asian Clearing Union. "Belarus recently requested to join this union, and we invite Russia to join as well," he said.[^cite_note-410] The [National Bank of Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Belarus), Russia's closest ally, has already become a member of the Asian Clearing Union.[^cite_note-411]

Launched in 2025, [Digital renminbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_renminbi) is already available in Russia and other CIS countries, said [Boris Titov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Titov), co-chairman of the Russian-Chinese Friendship Committee.[^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413]

Russia has become the leader among European countries surpassing Britain in terms of incoming cryptocurrency flows, according to a report by the analytical company Chainalysis for the period from July 2024 to June 2025. Analysts estimate the volume of cryptocurrencies that entered Russia during this period at \$376.3 billion. One reason for the rapid growth of the Russian cryptocurrency market has been cross-border settlements, primarily using the ruble-pegged [stablecoin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stablecoin) [A7A5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A7A5?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-414]

The share of payments in Russian rubles in Russia's foreign trade settlements in August 2025 once again reached an all-time high, reaching 55.2%, according to calculations based on data on the currency structure of foreign trade settlements on the Bank of Russia website. The share of payments in rubles for Russian exports was record high in August, rising to 56.3%. The share of payments in Russian rubles for imported goods and services to Russia in August was 54.1%. Payments in currencies of friendly countries accounted for 29.4% of exports from Russia in August. In payments for imports to Russia, this figure was 30.1% in August. The share of "toxic" currencies in payments for exports in August was 14.3%, and for imports to Russia, it was 15.7%.[^cite_note-415] Russia and Iran already conduct 80% of their mutual settlements in rials and rubles. [^cite_note-416] According to the Ministry of Economic Development, the share of national currencies in trade with CIS countries has already exceeded 85%, and with China it has come close to 95%.[^cite_note-417] In November 2025, Minister [Siluanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Siluanov) stated that the ruble and yuan now account for over 99% of trade settlements between Russia and China.[^cite_note-418]

A 2025 study said that an important constraint on the economy was “Russia’s dependence on hard currencies like the dollar and the euro, with only partial “yuanization” of its foreign trade transactions”.[^cite_note-419]

### Investments

See also: [International Investment Agreements of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Investment_Agreements_of_Russia)

[Russia](25391.md) [has concluded a number of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Investment_Agreements_of_Russia) [Bilateral Investment Treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilateral_Investment_Treaties) (BITs), [Treaties with Investment Provisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaties_with_Investment_Provisions?action=edit\&redlink=1) (TIPs) and [Investment Related Instruments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investment_Related_Instruments?action=edit\&redlink=1) (IRIs) according to the database of [UNCTAD](36587.md).[^cite_note-420] 65 agreements have entered into force.[^cite_note-421]

Russia recorded a trade surplus of US\$15.8 billion in 2013.[^cite_note-422]

In 2015, Russia main exports are oil and natural gas (62.8% of total exports), ores and metals (5.9%), chemical products (5.8%), machinery and transport equipment (5.4%) and food (4.7%). Others include: agricultural raw materials (2.2%) and textiles (0.2%).[^cite_note-423]

Russia top exports in 2021 were: Crude oil \$110.9b, Processed oil \$69.9b, gold \$17.3b, coal \$15.4b and natural gas \$7.3b.[^cite_note-worldbank.org-424]

Russia top imports in 2021 were: Transmission equipment \$10.7b, medication \$7.3b, tankers \$3.7b, parts and accessories for data processing 3.7b and storage units \$3.3b.[^cite_note-worldbank.org-424]

#### 2013–present

In 2017, Russian Federation's commercial services' shares of total exports and imports were 13.9% and 26.8%, respectively. Russian Federation had a trade-to-GDP ratio of 46.6% in 2017.[^cite_note-425] In 2013–2017, Russia had a trade surplus for goods, and a trade deficit for services. Since trade in goods is larger than trade in services, Russia had a significant trade surplus.[^cite_note-426] Trade is relatively important to the Russian economy: the ratio of Russia's goods trade (exports plus imports) to GDP has averaged about 40% in recent years, compared to 20% for the United States. In 2021, Russia ranked 13th among world goods exporters and 22nd among importers. According to Russian official sources, its goods exports totaled \$492 billion in 2021, up 46% from 2020 (not adjusting for inflation). Minerals, including oil and gas, made up nearly 45% of these exports. Goods imports rose by 27%, reaching \$294 billion in 2021. Machinery and mechanical appliances topped the list of imports, accounting for almost a third of Russia's goods imports. In services trade, Russia ranked 26th among world exporters and 19th among importers in 2020, the most recent year for which data is available. The country was a net importer of services, exporting \$49 billion worth of services and importing \$76 billion.[^cite_note-auto4-389]

According to the World Bank, imports of goods and services accounted for 21.3% of Russia's gross domestic product (GDP) in 2021,[^cite_note-427] while exports made up 30.9%.[^cite_note-428] Russia has [trade-to-GDP ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade-to-GDP_ratio) (trade openness) 49.26% [^cite_note-429] which is below the global average. In a December 2022 study, an economist from the Bank of Russia's Research and Forecasting Department found that Russia's import dependence is relatively low, does not exceed the median for other countries and the share of imports in most industries is lower than in other countries. The key explanation for this could be the low involvement of the Russian economy in global value supply chains and its focus on production of raw materials. However, 60% of Russia's imports come from the countries that have imposed sanctions against it.[^cite_note-auto1-182]

## Mergers and acquisitions

Between 1985 and 2018, almost 28,500 mergers or acquisitions have been announced in Russia. This cumulates to an overall value of around US\$984 billion which translates to RUB 5.456 billion. In terms of value, 2007 has been the most active year with US\$158 billion, whereas the number of deals peaked in 2010 with 3,684 (964 compared to the value record year 2007). Since 2010 value and numbers have decreased constantly and another wave of M\&A is expected.[^cite_note-430]
